It's just... always there.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it hard to concentrate on things, like following a conversation, reading, or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, that's a big part of why I decided to come in. My head just... it doesn't stay on task anymore. Or I'll walk all the way to the supply closet and completely forget what I went in there for. It's just hard to keep my focus on anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your movements or speech have slowed down, or that you feel restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I haven't noticed my speech slowing down or anything like that. And I don't feel restless or fidgety, either. I'm on my feet a lot for work, but I move around at a normal pace, I think. It's really just... that heavy feeling and the trouble focusing that I mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're not worth much, or blaming yourself for things more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I just... I keep telling myself that other guys would have adjusted by now and moved up. But I'm still just trying not to miss the small things at work. I can be sitting with my family, trying to act normal and enjoy the moment—and I do get satisfaction from it—but that weight is still there. It's the same everywhere, really. Even when I’m at work, just pushing my cleaning cart down an empty hallway... it's just always kind of hanging around in the background.\n\nDoctor: Is this heavy feeling something you experience most of the day, nearly every day, even when nothing stressful is happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. It's there most of the day, pretty much every day. It doesn't really matter if it's a good day or a stressful day, or if nothing at all is happening. It's just... always there.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it hard to concentrate on things, like following a conversation, reading, or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, that's a big part of why I decided to come in. My head just... it doesn't stay on task anymore. Like, I'll be doing inventory at work, or trying to study for this supervisor certification I've been looking at, and I just keep reading the same line over and over. Or I'll walk all the way to the supply closet and completely forget what I went in there for. It's just hard to keep my focus on anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your movements or speech have slowed down, or that you feel restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I haven't noticed my speech slowing down or anything like that. And I don't feel restless or fidgety, either. I'm on my feet a lot for work, but I move around at a normal pace, I think. It's really just... that heavy feeling and the trouble focusing that I mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're not worth much, or blaming yourself for things more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I just... I keep telling myself that other guys would have adjusted by now and moved up. But I'm still just trying not to miss the small things at work. It really makes me feel like maybe I'm just not cut out for anything more than this.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where even the things you usually enjoy, like watching a game or spending time with your kids, don't feel good at all?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed that. I still enjoy watching a game or spending time with my kids. Those things still feel good to me. It's not like I've lost interest in the stuff I like doing... it's really just the focus issues and feeling down on myself, like I was saying before.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t take as much pleasure or interest in activities you used to enjoy, even when you're doing them?\n\nPatient: I think that's kind of what you just asked... but no, I haven't noticed anything like that. Like I said, I still get the same enjoyment out of the things I like doing. It hasn't changed how much I care about my hobbies or anything. I'm still interested in all that stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble making decisions, even about small things, like what to eat or what to wear?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not so much about deciding what to eat or wear... it's more about just keeping my head on straight. That's actually why I came in. My head just doesn't stay on task anymore. Like, I'll be doing inventory at work, or trying to study for that supervisor certification, and I just... I keep reading the same line over and over. Or I'll walk all the way to the supply closet and completely forget what I went in there for. It's just hard to think straight or focus on anything.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
